Experiments on the VideoXum and MixTyVSum benchmarks show that RVLSM consistently improves over zero-shot VideoLLM baselines across multiple model settings and performance metrics, and is competitive with a state-of-the-art fine-tuned model for video-to-text summarization. Importantly, the named entities in the RVLSM's summaries align more closely with those detected in the ground-truth summaries than those detected by baseline and state-of-the-art methods, confirming the saliency of the event descriptions it generates.
  </details>

- **[Policy-Adaptive Credit Assignment for Spatial Reasoning with Verifiable Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26410)**  `arXiv:2610.26410`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hailiang Yang, Shengchao Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image-based spatial reasoning requires perception, decomposition, and inference, but the stage that limits accuracy can change during training. Answer-level rewards neither localize failures to individual stages nor provide discriminative group-relative feedback when all rollouts receive the same reward. Process supervision provides intermediate feedback, but fixed weights cannot adapt reward allocation to these changing bottlenecks. To address this gap, we propose CALIPER, an adaptive process-reward framework supported by SpatialTrace, a corpus of stage-decomposed spatial reasoning traces. CALIPER jointly estimates the associations between stage-wise reference coverage and answer correctness through within-group ridge regression, using these associations as utility proxies to gradually update reward weights. The resulting process reward supervises all rollout groups, including uniformly failing ones. Because utility estimates can become unreliable when answer-discriminative groups are scarce, a closed-loop harness monitors estimator reliability and validation performance to guide checkpoint rollback, rollout diversification, and early stopping. Extensive experiments across eight benchmarks and three backbones show that CALIPER achieves the highest mean under both one-shot and harness training, with consistent gains over fixed allocation across RL optimizers. Further analyses confirm the benefits of adaptive reward allocation and harness control, with favorable scaling in model/data size.
  </details>

- **[One Fact, Many Places: Redundant Evidence in Long Visual Documents Breaks Single-Gold Evaluation of Retrieval, Citation and Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26401)**  `arXiv:2610.26401`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiaokang Yuan, Binhui Yao, Luwei Yang, Ruimin Chen, Zeng Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Document understanding is increasingly judged on where an answer comes from, scored against annotated gold locations as if each fact lived in one place. In long visual documents, a fact often lives in several places: a chart value is restated in the text, a key figure reappears in the summary. We ask how often such copies occur in standard benchmarks and what they do to evaluation. We annotate 420 questions from MMLongBench-Doc and MMDocRAG with the locations that independently establish each required fact, checked by two independent reviewers. Copies are common and often cross-modal: 19.5% of MMLongBench-Doc test questions have a strict copy outside the gold, and in 28% of MMDocRAG questions a non-gold candidate alone supplies a required fact. Single-gold scoring then fails in three predictable ways. A retriever that finds a copy is scored as a miss: page recall@1 of text and visual retrievers is underestimated by 13-24 points on questions with a cross-page copy. A model that cites a copy is scored as citing wrongly: crediting equivalent evidence reverses 11% of pairwise orderings among 65 competitive MMDocRAG systems. Masking the gold region leaves the fact available elsewhere: with a copy in the input, 41-56% of correct answers across four models survive it yet fail once every annotated instance is masked. Set-level masking with automatically found copies closes 78% of the gap to masking every annotated instance at matched granularity (three models); an unverified key-string rule lowers survival at least as far but masks three times the area. We release the annotations and redundancy-aware metrics.
  </details>

- **[CARNet: End-to-End Coupled Association-Recognition Network for Fine-Grained Multi-Drone Collaborative Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26312)**  `arXiv:2610.26312`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhao Zhang, Ruigang Fu, Yu Zhang, Jiajun Xiang, Zixuan Xiao, Ping Zhong_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-drone collaborative detection aims to enhance target recognition by leveraging complementary observations from overlapping aerial viewpoints. Existing approaches typically follow a two-stage pipeline that first establishes cross-view correspondences and then performs fusion-based recognition. However, this decomposition suffers from inconsistent optimization objectives, redundant pairwise modeling, and irreversible error propagation, where incorrect associations inevitably undermine subsequent recognition performance. To overcome these limitations, we propose the Coupled Association-Recognition Network (CARNet), a unified framework that jointly optimizes cross-view target association and recognition fusion through a shared target-pair representation. To improve association reliability in densely populated scenes with visually similar targets, CARNet explicitly incorporates spatial cues into pairwise relation modeling. Moreover, we introduce two complementary causal-inspired intervention strategies: the first enhances cross-view association by emphasizing appearance-confusing pairs, while the second bolsters the robustness of fusion-based recognition by learning stable multi-view representations under incomplete visual evidence. In addition, we present MDMT-CD, a fine-grained extension of the MDMT dataset that enriches the original vehicle annotations with detailed category labels, providing the first benchmark specifically designed for multi-drone collaborative vehicle detection. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CARNet consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods. The MDMT-CD dataset and source code will be available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[From Cause to Effect: Generating Intermediate Event Chains in Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26275)**  `arXiv:2610.26275`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ridouane Ghermi, Vicky Kalogeiton, Ivan Laptev_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video-Language Models (VLMs) excel at visual recognition but struggle with causal reasoning. Current evaluations are flawed: multiple-choice QA allows shortcuts, and open-ended QA lacks robust automatic evaluation. We introduce Causal Chain Generation (CCG), a novel task where models have to generate the sequence of intermediate events connecting a cause and an effect. This structured setup demands genuine reasoning and enables reliable automatic scoring. We accompany this task with two complementary metrics: SODA-Embedding, which measures the semantic matching of a generated chain against a reference, and Causal-QA, which probes the chain's causal coherence by asking whether it contains the specific causal facts needed to reconstruct the narrative. We provide two test sets: SF20K-CC, a manually annotated diagnostic on 20 short films, and CMD-CC, a movie clip benchmark derived from audio descriptions. Our analysis reveals that state-of-the-art VLMs struggle with narrative causality understanding, frequently hallucinating or omitting key events. To address this, we fine-tune VLMs with GRPO using a composite reward that combines a format reward, SODA-Embedding, and Causal-QA accuracy, demonstrating substantial improvements over strong baselines.
  </details>

- **[Dynamics-Aware Sparse Attention for Efficient Autoregressive Video Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26262)**  `arXiv:2610.26262`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuanyu He, Zhuokun Chen, Yefei He, Zhiwei Tang, Jiasheng Tang, Yinghao Yu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Autoregressive Diffusion Transformers have emerged as a powerful paradigm for long video generation. However, they are often constrained by prohibitive computational overhead and substantial memory footprints. This inefficiency arises from dense computation mechanisms that allocate uniform processing resources across all spatiotemporal regions, failing to leverage the inherent temporal redundancy of video where motion is typically confined to sparse areas. In this paper, we propose Dynamics-Aware Sparse Attention (DASA), a unified framework that shifts from dense computation to sparse, motion-driven generation to eliminate both storage and computational redundancies. Inspired by video codec principles, our approach explicitly decouples video content into dynamic and static regions. To mitigate memory bottlenecks, we selectively compress the historical KV cache by retaining only dynamic features within the generated chunk. Furthermore, to reduce computational overhead, we employ a region-level computation allocation strategy based on localized dynamics. Additionally, a dynamics-guided distillation method is introduced to enhance performance via a lightweight distillation training process. Experiments on VBench demonstrate that our approach accelerates autoregressive video generation by 2.16x, while preserving high visual fidelity.
  </details>

- **[RoAR-VAE: Improving Latent Diffusability through Robust Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26216)**  `arXiv:2610.26216`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yang Yue, Fangyun Wei, Yitong Wang, Jinjing Zhao, Henry Zheng, Dong Chen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  An effective visual tokenizer must preserve image fidelity while producing latents that downstream diffusion models can learn efficiently. Prior work improves latent diffusability through semantic alignment or joint tokenizer--diffusion training. Revisiting REPA-E, a representative approach, we find that removing its diffusion objective and dedicated generative blocks while retaining semantic prediction from corrupted latents yields comparable downstream generation performance under a matched setting. This observation motivates a general principle of robust prediction: generation-relevant information should remain recoverable from corrupted latents. We instantiate this principle with two complementary objectives. RobustAlign predicts pretrained semantic features from corrupted latents to preserve high-level structure, while RobustRecon reconstructs the clean image to retain fine-grained spatial and appearance information. Together, they form Robust Alignment and Reconstruction VAE (RoAR-VAE). Our analysis suggests that robust prediction improves diffusability by making useful information more accessible in high-variance latent directions, which diffusion models tend to learn earlier. Empirically, RoAR-VAE improves average generation benchmark performance by 2.19 points over the strongest baseline tokenizer under the same training budget, while improving small-text reconstruction accuracy by 16.93 percentage points over FLUX.1-VAE. These results demonstrate that RoAR-VAE achieves a favorable balance between reconstruction fidelity and downstream generative learnability.
  </details>

- **[PnP-Transformer: A Neural Pose Solver for 6D Object Pose Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26096)**  `arXiv:2610.26096`  `cs.CV`  
  _Emirkan Burak Yilmaz, Yakup Genc_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present PnP-Transformer, a neural pose solver designed as an iterative-free, differentiable alternative to classical PnP-RANSAC algorithms. PnP-Transformer regresses the 6D object pose by processing predicted semi-dense 2D-3D correspondence fields alongside confidence scores, while mapping geometric inputs into a continuous latent space. To evaluate its practical utility, we integrate PnP-Transformer into a zero-shot 6D pose estimation pipeline, where the correspondence module and pose solver are trained separately. PnP-Transformer achieves a 41.2% mean AR score with low latency (0.46 s/obj) on seven core BOP benchmark datasets, surpassing all prior baselines (such as RayPose with 39.1% AR) but one (Co-op with 58.4% AR). When the pose solver is replaced with EPnP-RANSAC (similar to Co-op) while keeping the correspondence module unchanged, the RANSAC alternative outperforms PnP-Transformer on six of the seven datasets by reaching 52.2% mean AR at a cost of an additional 60 ms per object, revealing an accuracy-latency trade-off. Code and pretrained models are available:this https URL
  </details>

- **[DeOverlay: A Benchmark and Source-Anchored Flow for Video Overlay Removal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26081)**  `arXiv:2610.26081`  `cs.CV`  
  _Deshui Miao, Yuhang Ma, Shaoyong Jia, Wenzhao Gao, Yue Zhang, Shaohui Jiao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video overlay removal aims to recover scenes obscured by subtitles, titles, and logos while preserving surrounding content. Existing methods use temporal propagation or source-conditioned generation, but can leave residual overlays and distort scene structure when useful source information is obscured. We propose DeOverlay, a source-anchored flow model that reconstructs the clean video from the source latent with fixed source guidance. Specifically, we train a shared velocity network along paired source-to-clean paths using region-weighted flow matching, enabling successive cor- rections from intermediate states. To select local source cues, we develop visibility-normalized temporal aggregation that excludes masked positions and averages the remaining observations. Global source context and edit-region embeddings complement these cues through additive condi- tioning, retaining a single video-token sequence. To evaluate scene recovery across diverse overlay conditions, we introduce DVTE-Bench, comprising 1,631 paired videos across eight text-overlay categories, with OCR-screened clean targets, frame-aligned pixel masks, and a common evaluation protocol. Experiments on the complete release show that DeOverlay outperforms four baselines on all five reconstruction and perceptual metrics, reducing Mask LPIPS and Mask VFID by 58.3% and 70.3%, respectively, relative to the strongest evaluated baseline. Additional qualitative examples demonstrate the removal of graphic logos and stickers. We will make the complete benchmark, evaluation code, and test outputs availabl. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Toolkit for Facial Deidentification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26079)**  `arXiv:2610.26079`  `cs.CV`  
  _Esteban Leiva-Montenegro, Matthieu Pillot, Manfred Gonzalez-Hernandez, Vitomir Struc, Peter Peer, Blaž Meden_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  This work presents a novel open-source toolkit designed for the evaluation of face deidentification techniques with available evaluation models. The framework provides a complete evaluation pipeline, including preprocessing, deidentification, and performance assessment, based on data utility, image quality, and identity protection metrics. It supports a variety of datasets, enabling researchers to easily benchmark new deidentification techniques. Results show the effectiveness of some techniques on maintaining privacy while preserving image utility. The toolkit is aimed at unifying future research by providing a standard evaluation framework for privacy-enhancing technologies in facial biometrics. Implementation is available atthis http URL.
  </details>

- **[Risk Decomposition for Hallucination-Aware Video Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26069)**  `arXiv:2610.26069`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chenyan Zheng, Fan Wu, Ruibo Li, Jiaxing Li, Minghao Yang, Bang Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Despite achieving high visual fidelity, video models still suffer from physical hallucinations, limiting their reliability as world simulators. Existing methods leverage model-internal or external signals to improve physical plausibility, but their roles and limitations require a unified explanation. We propose a two-dimensional physical-hallucination risk framework that defines low-density sampling risk and high-density drifting risk. The first risk captures generations assigned low likelihood by the video model, for example, a ball suddenly teleporting despite the model favoring continuous motion, while the second risk captures physical inconsistencies in high-likelihood generations, for example, a ball bouncing progressively higher without additional energy. Our analysis explains how model-internal signals steer sampling toward higher-density regions of the learned distribution to address low-density sampling risk. However, higher model likelihood does not guarantee physical plausibility, motivating external guidance to address high-density drifting risk. Based on this framework, we develop Hallucination-Aware Sampler (HA-Sampler), which applies external physical guidance within latent refinement to generate physically plausible videos, and HA-Scorer, which derives a likelihood-based signal from the generator itself for evaluating generated videos using continuous normalizing flow (CNF), with coarse ODE integration and finite-difference divergence estimates. Experiments on Physics-IQ and VideoPhy benchmarks support our theoretical analysis and demonstrate that our methods outperform the compared sampling and scoring SOTA baselines.
  </details>

- **[AVTime: Reinforcing Dense Audio-Visual Captions with Bidirectional Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26046)**  `arXiv:2610.26046`  `cs.CV`  
  _Mingyang Wu, Kaituo Feng, Bohao Li, Kaixiong Gong, Zihao Yin, Xiangyu Yue_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Dense temporal audio-visual captioning is important for multimodal video understanding and generation. However, prior work is constrained by three main limitations: (1) the lack of a dataset combining high temporal resolution, longer videos, and bidirectional alignment between time and events; (2) the reliance of most existing RL methods on checklists; and (3) the lack of a benchmark that bidirectionally evaluates event semantic correctness and temporal accuracy. To address these challenges, we propose: (1) AVTime-50K, a high-quality 50K-scale dataset with temporally precise, semantically accurate captions and longer videos; (2) AVTime, a state-of-the-art model optimized with a novel Bidirectional Temporal-Semantic Alignment (BiTSA) reward that imposes explicit bidirectional constraints between time and events; and (3) AVTime-Bench, a specialized benchmark that introduces a novel bidirectional evaluation of temporal accuracy and semantic correctness. Our code, models, and datasets are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[After the Federation: Benchmarking Knowledge Portability to Heterogeneous Newcomers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26026)**  `arXiv:2610.26026`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xinyuan Zhao_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Once historical clients become unavailable, a federation must serve newcomers through the artifact it leaves behind. How much of its knowledge remains useful in an architecture chosen afterward? We introduce FEDNBO, a seven-dataset benchmark that separates knowledge supply, policy identification, and private-model compilation under disjoint data roles. Controlled studies expose inflated oracle gains, architecture-dependent transfer reversals, and a gap between teacher accuracy and private-model utility. Ordinary public-response distillation provides a competitive reference without public task labels or newcomer samples: gains over strengthened local training are 23.52 and 16.62 percentage points on 300 CelebA identities and 300 FedEMNIST writers, respectively. In a complete-pipeline FedEMNIST comparison, this reference reaches 97.13% accuracy with 12.4 seconds of compilation per architecture, versus 97.03% and 67.5 seconds for a HeteroFedTTA reconstruction at the same epoch budget. Equal-count public-input interventions extend support sensitivity to trainable visual backbones. Together, these results establish an auditable benchmark and a strong reference for evaluating the utility, cost and reliability of post-federation knowledge transfer.
  </details>

- **[MIRA: A Multimodal Illustrated Deep Research Agent for Controllable Reports](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25926)**  `arXiv:2610.25926`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hongrui Wu, Kaiwen Tuo, Congcong Wang, Gen Li, Shuai Dong, Xinlei Yu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Deep research agents are increasingly capable of searching the open web and synthesizing evidence, yet they remain poorly suited to controllable report generation in multimodal deep research. This task requires agents to handle interleaved textual and visual inputs, gather multimodal evidence through multi-step web search, and deliver comprehensive illustrated reports under user-specified requirements. Existing systems face two limitations. During reasoning, they often underuse images discovered along the search trajectory; during generation, they tend to produce fixed-form answers with limited control over report granularity, multimodal composition, and evidence grounding. We introduce MIRA, a Multimodal Illustrated Deep Research Agent for controllable reports. MIRA integrates textual and visual evidence throughout research and writing. At its core, MIRA-Harness translates user requirements into generation contracts and connects four stages: evidence collection, report submission, feedback-driven revision, and final delivery. Building on this harness, MIRA-Data constructs high-quality multi-hop multimodal research trajectories, and we post-train foundation models to operate within the workflow. Using Qwen3.6-35B-A3B, MIRA achieves state-of-the-art performance among open agents on 7 representative, established multimodal-search and deep-research-agent benchmarks, while producing user-aligned illustrated reports with traceable textual and visual evidence.
  </details>

- **[Hihaze: One-Step Diffusion for High-Resolution Real-World Image Dehazing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25832)**  `arXiv:2610.25832`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zheng Chen, Yuchen Huang, Xiaoyang Liu, Jue Gong, Linghe Kong, Yulun Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion models have recently achieved encouraging results in real-world image dehazing. However, restoring high-resolution images remains challenging due to spatially non-uniform haze and inconsistent predictions across local patches. To address these issues, we propose Hihaze, a one-step diffusion framework for high-resolution real-world dehazing. Hihaze adopts two haze-aware modulation modules: attentive skip compensation (ASC) and a spatial degradation adapter (SDA). ASC selectively transfers reliable encoder information to the variational autoencoder (VAE) decoder, preserving structural details while suppressing haze-degraded features. SDA extracts multi-scale spatial degradation representations and injects them into the U-Net to guide restoration according to local haze variations. Furthermore, we design a training-free global-guided patch fusion (GPF) method. It uses a global prediction to align patch appearance and downweight unreliable predictions, followed by structure-guided detail compensation. Notably, this training-free design also makes GPF a plug-and-play module for other dehazing models. Experiments across four real-world benchmarks show that Hihaze surpasses state-of-the-art approaches in both visual quality and inference speed. Specifically, it delivers PSNR improvements of 3.62~dB on Dense-Haze and 3.69~dB on NH-HAZE compared with the strongest baselines. Code:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[See the Future, Classify the Present: Stochastic Latent World Modeling for Hyperspectral Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25781)**  `arXiv:2610.25781`  `cs.CV`  
  _Muhammad Ahmad, Gemine Vivone, Ronny Hänsch, Ahmed Sohaib, Manuel Mazzara, Danfeng Hong_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Hyperspectral image classification (HSIC) relies on static spatial-spectral representations learned from labeled samples, limiting its ability to model structured dependencies in the latent representation space. To address this limitation, this work proposes Stochastic Latent World Modeling (LWM), a trajectory-based representation-learning framework that enriches classification through stochastic latent-state prediction. Given a hyperspectral patch, LWM tokenizes and encodes spectral and spatial information separately using dedicated transformer branches, then fuses their outputs into a unified representation. This representation is further organized into an environment state that captures center, neighborhood, spectral, and global contextual information. A stochastic latent transition module then predicts multiple future latent states through Gaussian parameterization and reparameterization, with recurrent refinement used to model dependencies across successive states. The resulting latent trajectory is adaptively aggregated by a rollout planner to construct a planned representation, which is integrated with the base representation for final classification. By extending static spatial-spectral encoding with stochastic multi-step latent-state modeling, LWM enables the classifier to exploit structured dependencies in the representation space and provides richer features for HSIC. Across five benchmark datasets, LWM achieves overall accuracies ranging from 89.21% to 99.52%, demonstrating consistent classification performance under limited-label settings.
  </details>

- **[VHA: VQA-based Hallucination Assessment for Generative Image Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25770)**  `arXiv:2610.25770`  `cs.CV`  
  _Donghun Ryou, Inju Ha, Jaeyoo Park, Bohyung Han_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generative image super-resolution produces visually realistic high-resolution images, but may also introduce hallucinations by adding unsupported content or altering existing details. Because existing super-resolution metrics fail to measure whether generated details remain grounded in the reference image, we present a hallucination assessment technique based on bidirectional visual question answering (VQA), which we term VQA-based Hallucination Assessment (VHA). The proposed approach uses a vision-language model (VLM) to determine whether the semantic content and low-level visual evidence in a super-resolved image are supported by its ground-truth counterpart. To this end, it constructs question--answer pairs from each image and evaluates them on both images. Discrepancies between the resulting scores in both directions reveal missing or altered ground-truth information and unsupported additions. However, obtaining these scores requires computationally expensive VLM inference. We therefore develop VHA+, a learned, lightweight model that directly predicts VHA scores from ground-truth and super-resolved image pairs. We construct a hallucination benchmark, VHA-bench, through a human study and show that both VHA and VHA+ correlate highly with human judgments of hallucination. The code, benchmark, and model weights are available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[LEVI: Benchmarking Visual Identity Fidelity with Verifiable Low-Entropy Constructs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25716)**  `arXiv:2610.25716`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiao Ma, Zhonghao Zhan, Hamed Haddadi, Qi Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Modern generative models can produce visually plausible images while violating exact identity constraints. Evaluating such failures requires distinguishing violations of the requested identity from permissible variation in appearance. We introduce Low-Entropy Visual Identity (LEVI), which characterizes visual identities determined by compact facts and explicit rules. The low-entropy requirement concerns identity uncertainty, while appearance may remain diverse. We formulate identity fidelity in terms of the probability that a conditional image distribution assigns to the identities permitted by the prompt. Visual Identity Spaces (VIS) formalize this distinction by specifying admissible identity values and which different realizations express the same identity. Building on this formulation, we develop Verifiable Visual Grammar (VVG), a declarative language whose typed primitives and compositional constraints describe identity-bearing structures. Using this grammar, we build a benchmark for image generation. It assesses each output against the task's identity requirements, drawing evidence from the final pixels. The curated benchmark contains 1,375 tasks spanning 120 base identity spaces across six domains. Among ten evaluated model configurations, the highest whole-task pass rate is 58.69%. These results reveal a persistent gap between visually plausible images and faithful realization of their specified identities. Project page (forthcoming):this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Visual Memory and Textual Alignment for Cross-Modal Few-Shot Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25641)**  `arXiv:2610.25641`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tianqi Wei, Zhi Chen, Xin Yu, Scott Chapman, Zi Huang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Few-shot segmentation (FSS) aims to segment target objects with limited annotated examples. Recent studies have begun to explore large foundation models to improve generalization for this task. In particular, SAM 3 provides strong visual representations and supports text-guided segmentation. However, its performance remains limited in specialized domains, where semantic prompts and visual representations are often poorly aligned. To address this challenge, we propose ViTA-SAM, a framework built upon SAM 3 that integrates visual memory and textual features through cross-modal alignment. Specifically, we construct a visual memory module that encodes support features together with their corresponding masks, providing support-aware visual guidance for query prediction. In parallel, we leverage the text encoder of SAM 3 to extract semantic features as complementary priors. To bridge the gap between semantic and visual representations, we introduce a cross-modal alignment module that conditions textual features on memory-enhanced visual representations, yielding more consistent and visually grounded predictions. In addition, we propose a pseudo-label refinement strategy that updates the memory with predicted query masks, enabling query-adaptive representation learning. Extensive experiments on multiple benchmarks show that ViTA-SAM achieves the best overall performance among the compared methods, outperforming the strongest competing method by over 5% in average mIoU.
  </details>

- **[GroundedVDR: Grounding Video-to-Web Evidence Dependencies in Video Deep Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25493)**  `arXiv:2610.25493`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiyuan Liang, Yu Wang, Zuhao Yang, Ziting Wang, Duo Li, Sudong Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video large multimodal models have improved at identifying cross-frame visual evidence and conducting open-web retrieval, as reflected in recent video deep research (VDR) benchmarks. However, existing VDR benchmarks primarily evaluate tool-use trajectories and final responses without verifying whether a model actually uses the temporal video information or the retrieved web evidence. To fill this gap, we introduce \method{}, an evidence-grounded benchmark with three features. First, it combines human review with restricted-access probing across multiple models to exclude questions answerable from parametric knowledge or a single information source. Second, each question carries structured video-to-web evidence annotations that link the relevant video segments to their supporting web evidence. Third, its evaluation protocol separately scores video grounding, web evidence grounding, and final-answer correctness. Across eight multimodal agent systems, the best reaches 76.0\% answer accuracy, yet none recovers the complete required web evidence for more than 8\% of questions. Our benchmark is publicly available at \url{this https URL}.
  </details>

- **[PCXR-Copilot: Device-aware pediatric chest radiographic analysis with segmentation, suppression, and edema-related cues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25486)**  `arXiv:2610.25486`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xueyang Li, Alec Girten, Peng Zeng, Mingze Jiang, Shaoyang Huang, Gelei Xu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Patients admitted to pediatric ICU and cardiac ICU often require intensive perioperative support and frequent chest radiography to monitor support devices, extravascular lung water, and body-wall edema. Radiographic interpretation and analysis is often difficult due to superimposed anatomic and iatrogenic structures that obscure clinically relevant signals. While deep learning can provide decision support, translation to pediatric radiographs is still limited by high device burden and scarcity of pediatric datasets with clinically grounded device annotations. To address this gap, we first introduce PediaDeviceSeg-1K, a curated dataset of 1,000 anterior-posterior radiographs of pediatric CICU patients with clinically grounded, fine-grained annotations of common ICU devices. To our best knowledge, this is the first publicly available pediatric CICU CXR resource with clinically grounded device annotations for device-focused benchmarking. Building on PediaDeviceSeg-1K, we further present PCXR-Copilot, a device-aware pipeline that outputs lung and device masks, optional device-suppressed views, and edema-related visualization maps and scalar indices. The full pipeline has been integrated and runs in real time within the TWIST platform at Boston Children's Hospital, demonstrating feasibility for real-world clinical deployment.
  </details>

- **[UBD-Bench: Benchmarking and Analyzing Unimodal Branch Degradation in Multimodal Perception for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25448)**  `arXiv:2610.25448`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ang Liu, Xiang Gu, Ruikai Li, Qiyue Wang, Yizhe Fan, Hao Shan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal perception improves autonomous-driving performance by combining complementary sensor information. However, stronger multimodal predictions do not necessarily imply stronger representations in each modality-specific branch. Such branch-level weaknesses can remain hidden in conventional output-level evaluation. We introduce UBD-Bench, a systematic benchmark for studying unimodal branch degradation (UBD) in multimodal perception. UBD-Bench uses a Paired Frozen-Branch Probe to compare the task-relevant representation quality of the same modality after independent unimodal training and multimodal joint training under matched decoding conditions. Across four autonomous-driving perception tasks and 10 models, we find that Camera branches consistently degrade after multimodal joint training, whereas LiDAR branches exhibit weaker and more model-dependent changes, revealing a pronounced modality asymmetry. Hierarchical probing further shows that Camera-branch degradation extends from the visual backbone through the Camera-to-BEV pathway and is most severe at the pre-fusion Camera BEV representation. Guided by this diagnosis, we introduce unimodal-to-multimodal distillation at the degraded representation. This intervention consistently improves both Camera-branch Probe performance and full-model performance across all four tasks. These results show that multimodal gains can coexist with degraded unimodal representations, highlighting branch-level representation quality as an important dimension for evaluating and improving multimodal perception.
  </details>

- **[From Label Priors to Task Evidence: Efficient Long-Video Frame Selection via Bayesian GRPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25418)**  `arXiv:2610.25418`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shuochen Chang, Bingjie Gao, Qingyang Liu, Qianli Ma, Yibo Miao, Haonan Zhao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) face significant scalability challenges in long-video understanding due to quadratic attention complexity and context window constraints. Existing retrieval-based approaches typically rely on supervised learning from static keyframe annotations. However, these label priors often fail to align with the actual information required by downstream MLLMs for complex reasoning, creating a misalignment between the training target and inference needs. To bridge this gap, we propose a probabilistic framework that reformulates frame selection as latent-variable inference. We first initialize a lightweight policy using supervised learning to capture general visual saliency. Then, we treat a frozen MLLM as a task environment and use its answer likelihood as dense task evidence to refine the policy via Bayesian Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO). Within this formulation, the negative log-likelihood (NLL) over ground-truth answer tokens provides a principled dense reward, while a prior-matching KL term preserves semantic consistency with the elicited label prior. This process transitions the selection principle from static label priors to task evidence. Extensive experiments on Video-MME, MLVU, and LongVideoBench demonstrate that our selector achieves competitive accuracy with MLLM-based scorers while retaining the inference efficiency of lightweight methods. The selector is trained once with a frozen MLLM environment and transfers directly to multiple downstream MLLMs without retraining. Code is available at \url{this http URL}.
  </details>

- **[ATLAS: Attention-Guided Test-Time Latent Selection for Compositional Visual Autoregressive Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25390)**  `arXiv:2610.25390`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hossein Shahabadi, Raya Namazi, Mahdieh Soleymani Baghshah_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual autoregressive models produce convincing images but can misassign attributes and spatial relations. Searching multiple generation paths can improve composition, yet completing every candidate is costly. We introduce ATLAS, a training-free framework that uses the frozen generator's internal attention to guide search before final images are available. Within-prompt diagnostics identify attention-derived scores that predict final compositional preferences on Infinity, with category-dependent transfer to Switti. ATLAS uses these on-the-fly signals to progressively concentrate computation on promising trajectories. It maintains multiple coarse alternatives, prunes candidates without decoding at intermediate scales, and reserves image-space evaluation for a final choice among partial trajectories. ATLAS scales effectively on both backbones, achieving higher mean scores on the evaluated T2I-CompBench and GenEval categories as more computation is devoted to search. At the largest tested budget, the targeted T2I-CompBench mean improves by over 10% relative to each backbone. Separate profiles show over 50% less generation time than completing and evaluating all candidates at equal initial counts, alongside lower peak allocated GPU memory. Relative time savings are larger at the higher tested candidate count. Together, these results support scalable compositional search: additional sampling expands exploration, while attention-guided selection limits continuation cost without parameter updates or gradient-based optimization.
  </details>

- **[Only Means Only: what a harmonization benchmark score averages over](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25387)**  `arXiv:2610.25387`  `cs.CV`  
  _Imad Buljić, Delaida Muminović_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image harmonization methods adjust the colour and lighting of an object pasted into a photograph so that it looks native to its new scene, and they are ranked by average error on one benchmark whose test images are real photographs with one object recoloured. One photograph can yield several such test images, and the benchmark promises that no photograph appears in both the training and the test split. We find that 1,399 photographs do, and the composites built on them are a quarter of the test set. Duplicate checks over file names or bytes miss this, because every composite is a different file. On that quarter the three methods we score end to end read two to five times lower error than on the rest, but most of the gap is composition rather than the sharing: the shared composites come almost entirely from one of the benchmark's four source collections, and inside that collection the gap falls to about one and a half times. An official score is therefore an average over two populations, mixed in a proportion the benchmark does not report. We release a test list of 5,581 composites whose construction identities do not appear in training, with four confirmed near-duplicate pairs disclosed rather than removed, adoptable without retraining at the price of one rescore per checkpoint. We leave open whether the sharing itself changes what a model learns.
  </details>

- **[Painting Change Map as guidance for generating painting processes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25369)**  `arXiv:2610.25369`  `cs.CV`  
  _Markus Pobitzer, Chang Liu, Niccolò Biondi, Nicu Sebe_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Digital art generation has undergone a massive paradigm shift, moving from static image synthesis to dynamic video generation that can mimic the human painting process. While existing models can synthesize visually stunning time-lapses of paintings being created or "unpainted," they largely treat the creative process as a black box. Current conditioning mechanisms are either too rigid, relying on static geometric cues like Canny edges or depth maps, or too holistic, utilizing simple scalar progress variables that offer no localized control. Consequently, users lack the agency to steer how a painting evolves over time. In this work, we argue that true control over the video generation of artwork requires a joint understanding of two fundamental dimensions: where an artist acts, and when those actions take place. To achieve this, we introduce the Painting Change Map, a novel spatio-temporal control mechanism that explicitly tracks normalized structural changes across a 1,000-timestep continuum, a resolution sufficient to capture fine-grained stroke-level changes while remaining computationally tractable. By mapping localized visual differences into an encoded RGB space, our representation enables fine-grained guidance for both the forward painting and reverse unpainting processes. Extensive benchmarking demonstrates the efficacy of our approach. We outperform existing control baselines and achieve competitive or superior results compared to state-of-the-art painting methods. Furthermore, under oracle conditions, our method surpasses all baselines by a significant margin. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SENRI: Understanding the Space-Event Nexus in Real-Life Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25309)**  `arXiv:2610.25309`  `cs.CV`  
  _Kehan Lan, Kaining Ying, Henghui Ding, Kaipeng Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Human memories are organized in a way that connects events with their corresponding spatial contexts. An AI assistant should also be able to memorize and recall the nexus of events and spaces. Thus we introduce SENRI, a task that studies this space--event connection in long, continuous real-life egocentric video. It contains six task types in two directions: Event$\rightarrow$Space, which recovers spatial knowledge from event cues, and Space$\rightarrow$Event, which retrieves events from regions and trajectories. To evaluate this, we build SENRI-Bench, containing 500 manually annotated multiple-choice questions over 10 uninterrupted self-recorded videos with around 25.3 hours of content. Also, we evaluate three typical long video understanding paradigms: direct perception, evidence seeking, and persistent memory. The task remains challenging across all three paradigms, highlighting substantial room for improvement. Then we show two representative failure modes of existing memory methods and address these evidence gaps with SENRI-Agent, a controller that uses Target-Guided Visual Check (TGVC) to propose local visual check tasks for parallel verifiers and integrate the reports into a working memory to help with reasoning. Built upon existing memory systems, SENRI-Agent improves the performance of existing baselines without modifying their stored memories. We hope SENRI will provide valuable insights into how real-life egocentric understanding should be evaluated, reveal the gaps in existing approaches, and offer a possible direction for improvement.
  </details>

- **[Visual Scaffolding: Toward Native Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25247)**  `arXiv:2610.25247`  `cs.CV`  
  _Run Shao, Zhaoyang Zhang, Ziyu Li, Linrui Xu, Ji Qi, Xiu Su, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The success of chain-of-thought (CoT) in complex reasoning can be attributed to a textual scaffolding effect: explicit intermediate results can be examined, reused, and composed to support subsequent reasoning, much as scaffolding supports a building under construction. However, mainstream multimodal LLMs lack an equivalent scaffold for visual distinctions that are difficult to express in concise verbal descriptions. We therefore ask: Can native visual representations become first-class citizens alongside language, providing a visual scaffold for reasoning? We define native multimodal reasoning as reasoning in which native visual representations and language jointly carry reusable intermediate states. As a concrete implementation, the visual scaffolding mechanism we proposed lets the MLLM autonomously initiate a retrieval action at any reasoning step. A lightweight retriever selects native visual features, which are then incorporated directly into the evolving CoT and remain available to subsequent steps. To evaluate this mechanism under controlled visual demands, we introduce NMR-Bench, comprising Burden Counting and Path Tracing. With 88M added parameters (approximately 2%) on Qwen3-VL-4B-Thinking, our model achieves 66.43% and 72.50% accuracy, respectively, surpassing the evaluated GPT-5.5 and Gemini 3.1 Pro baselines. Controlled interventions show that changing selected visual content affects task accuracy and can redirect subsequent visual selection even when intervening text is held fixed. These findings show that native visual states can function as intermediate reasoning material and point toward reusable scaffolds for additional modalities.
  </details>

- **[Map2Vid: Embodied Navigation Video Generation with Human-Friendly Motion Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25223)**  `arXiv:2610.25223`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yulin Dai, Yuqian Fu, Tianwen Qian, Zhen Xing, Bojia Zi, Xiaoling Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Controllable video generation has recently achieved impressive visual quality, yet existing control paradigms remain poorly suited to embodied navigation. Text prompts are spatially ambiguous, while camera-trajectory control relies on low-level motion specifications that are unintuitive for human users and difficult to manipulate. In this work, we introduce Map2Vid, a novel task that reformulates embodied navigation video generation as a BEV-map-to-video synthesis problem, together with a large-scale benchmark for systematic evaluation. By using BEV maps, which provide a visual representation of global scene layout and intended navigation routes, Map2Vid enables human-friendly navigation video generation. To support this task, we construct a dedicated dataset with over 100K navigation sequences across 61 indoor scenes, each paired with egocentric videos, BEV maps, camera trajectories and language instructions. We further develop a unified framework that integrates multiple control signals, together with a curriculum training strategy that progressively weakens non-map inputs, enabling flexible inference under both full conditioning and map-only input. Extensive experiments across multiple foundation generation models demonstrate that controllable navigation video generation via BEV map is a viable and promising direction.
  </details>

- **[Image Dehazing as In-Context Tabular Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25142)**  `arXiv:2610.25142`  `cs.CV`  
  _Le-Anh Tran_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image dehazing is traditionally addressed through handcrafted physical priors or task-specific deep learning models, often requiring carefully designed architectures or extensive model training. Recent advances in tabular foundation models (TFMs), however, offer an alternative learning paradigm in which continuous relationships can be inferred directly from contextual examples without task-specific model training. Motivated by this capability, this paper investigates whether single-image dehazing can be effectively reformulated as an in-context tabular regression problem and introduces DehazeTab, a framework that estimates atmospheric transmission from structured image-patch representations. Specifically, overlapping patches extracted from hazy images are transformed into tabular samples using handcrafted haze-related descriptors, pretrained visual embeddings, or their hybrid combinations, while corresponding transmission patches are summarized as scalar regression targets. A pretrained TFM then performs in-context regression using labeled reference patches to predict transmission values for unseen images without any further fine-tuning. These predictions are spatially aggregated into a transmission map, refined through guided filtering, and incorporated into the atmospheric scattering model to recover haze-free images. Experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate competitive restoration performance and reveal that physically motivated handcrafted features can outperform pretrained visual embeddings, while hybrid representations offer complementary benefits. Further analyses examine the effects of feature representations, patch selection, and contextual reference configurations on restoration quality. Beyond image dehazing, the findings highlight the potential of TFMs as an alternative paradigm for addressing low-level vision tasks through structured and in-context inference.
  </details>

- **[Think Visually Across Space and Time: Grounded Reasoning for Video Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25090)**  `arXiv:2610.25090`  `cs.CV`  
  _Junwei Wen, Deshui Miao, Guangming Lu, Ming-Hsuan Yang, Xin Li, Wenjie Pei_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video Reasoning Segmentation (VRS) requires a model to identify an implicitly described target and predict its pixel-level mask trajectory, while resolving the spatial relations and temporal events that determine the answer. Text-only Chain-of-Thought (CoT) can explain a target choice without making the underlying visual references explicit, leaving a \emph{video reference gap} between linguistic inference and the evidence distributed across frames. We introduce \textbf{Temporal Chain of Video Primitives (TCoVP)}, a spatiotemporally grounded form of Video CoT that organizes query-relevant observations into a chronological reasoning chain. Each primitive associates an entity reference with temporal support and timestamped spatial evidence, and the chain interleaves these references with language to guide reasoning about which entity is relevant, when the queried event holds, and where its evidence appears. The selected target primitives then provide box prompts and temporal support for video mask prediction. We construct TCoVP-SFT-15k and TCoVP-RL-13k to learn this representation through supervised fine-tuning and chain-aligned reinforcement learning. Extensive experiments across five VRS benchmarks and three model backbones demonstrate the strong performance of TCoVP, highlighting the effectiveness of organizing spatial and temporal evidence within the reasoning process. All models and code will be publicly released. Project page: \url{this https URL}
  </details>

- **[Sufficient Frame Subsets for Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25084)**  `arXiv:2610.25084`  `cs.CV`  
  _Lahiru Wijayasingha_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Understanding when during a video a model makes its critical observations is essential for interpreting video-understanding systems. However, existing attribution methods often spread evidence diffusely across near-duplicate frames. We introduce Sufficient Frame Subsets: compact sets of frames in which all other frames are masked, yet each subset independently preserves the model's prediction. We evaluate our method on two complementary benchmarks, UCF101 and Something-Something v2, using both CNN- and transformer-based architectures. Our results show that the proposed algorithm identifies highly compact and effective frame subsets for explaining model decisions. We also find that models capable of relying on a greater number of sufficient frame subsets tend to perform better, possibly because they are more robust, consistent with prior research. Finally, the extracted frame subsets help us analyze model failure cases, reveal which aspects of a video the model has learned to emphasize, and identify opportunities for improving future training procedures.
  </details>

- **[MoWE: Spatially Adaptive Mixture-of-Wavelet-Experts Pooling for Ultrasound Lesion Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25074)**  `arXiv:2610.25074`  `cs.CV`  
  _Reaz Hassan Joarder, Sidratul Tanzila Tasmi, Alif Arshad Bakshi, Md. Bakhtiar Hasan, Md Hasanul Kabir_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present Mixture-of-Wavelet-Experts (MoWE), a spatially adaptive routing mechanism that replaces max-pooling and strided convolutions in U-Net architectures for ultrasound lesion segmentation. Standard pooling operations suppress high-frequency spatial details, including potential boundary cues in speckled regions. Recent frequency-domain methods preserve some of these details, but a single wavelet family applies the same spectral bias across the image. MoWE instead uses a context-aware router to assign local regions to fixed wavelet families according to local texture and global feature context. The decoder reuses the encoder's spatial routing maps during the inverse transform. Learnable channel-wise gates rescale the high-frequency subbands before reconstruction. We evaluate MoWE with U-Net on four ultrasound lesion benchmarks (BUSI, TN3K, UDIAT, BUS-UCLM) and one cardiac dataset (CAMUS), and with nnU-Net on BUSI. On BUSI, MoWE improves U-Net Dice from 72.59\% to 74.55\% and reduces HD95 from 47.01 to 43.49\,px. With nnU-Net, MoWE improves Dice from 80.18\% to 81.97\% and reduces ASSD from 20.07 to 17.25\,px. On U-Net, MoWE reduces trainable parameters by 6.4\%.
  </details>

- **[PRET is a few-shot system for pan-cancer recognition without example training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25054)**  `arXiv:2610.25054`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yi Li, Ziyu Ning, Tianqi Xiang, Qixiang Zhang, Zhihao Lin, Min Yi, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pathological examination stands as the cornerstone in cancer diagnosis, impacting millions worldwide annually. With the shortage of pathologists globally, artificial intelligence (AI) has emerged rapidly to automate the diagnostics process. However, conventional AI models require substantial labeled data for each disease, posing huge challenges in scalability and practicality. Therefore, we introduce PRET (pan-cancer recognition without examples training), a few-shot system to achieve flexible, scalable, and effective cancer recognition across diverse organs, hospitals and tasks without training. Evaluated on 23 international benchmarks comprising 4,484 whole-slide images, our method outperforms existing approaches across 20 tasks, achieving over 97% area under the curve on 15 benchmarks with a maximum improvement of 36.76%. Notably, PRET delivers clinical-grade diagnostic performance in lymph node metastasis detection using only eight slide examples, outperforming 11 pathologists. By offering a flexible and cost-effective solution for pan-cancer recognition, PRET paves the way for accessible and equitable AI-based pathology systems, particularly benefiting minority populations and underserved regions.
  </details>

- **[Goal Images Are Control Targets: Spatially-Grounded Goal-Image Synthesis for Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24885)**  `arXiv:2610.24885`  `cs.CV`  
  _Riddhi Chatterjee, Ritish Shrirao, Kushagra Mehrotra, Viswanath Gopalakrishnan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Goal-conditioned manipulation policies are steered by a goal image, a picture of the desired post-manipulation scene synthesized from the current observation and a language instruction. The prevailing assumption is that better image-editing quality yields better goals, so goal generators are built and compared using general image-editing metrics. We show this assumption is false. Across two independent goal-conditioned policies on CALVIN, the image editors that top aggregate editing-quality leaderboards (Gemini 3 Pro Image and GPT Image 1.5) produce worse control targets than our proposed far smaller finetuned open models, and the gap widens sharply for the multi-step (long-horizon) tasks robots actually face. A per-dimension analysis explains why. Downstream policy success is governed almost entirely by manipulation adherence (selecting the right object and the right final state; Spearman $\rho \approx 0.9$ with success), while photorealism and aesthetic fidelity, precisely where large proprietary editors excel, are uncorrelated with success ($\rho \approx 0$). This dissociation holds on both CALVIN and the real-world-aligned SIMPLER-Bridge benchmark. We then present SGE-Goal, a spatially-grounded decomposed editing framework that predicts initial and final 2D edit-region masks before goal-image synthesis. SGE-Goal turns open base models (FLUX.1 Kontext, FLUX.2 klein) into the strongest goal generators we evaluate on CALVIN, and second only to Gemini 3 Pro Image on SIMPLER-Bridge. Base models fine-tuned with SGE-Goal also hold up under chaining far better than the best proprietary editor, sustaining roughly seven times the success rate at the longest (five-step) chains. We release code, datasets, checkpoints, and a human-aligned diagnostic evaluator. Our results argue that goal generators must be selected by downstream utility, not by editing-quality leaderboards.
  </details>

- **[PCHMR: Empowering and Benchmarking Human Mesh Recovery in Privacy-Constrained Real-World Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24841)**  `arXiv:2610.24841`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zeyu Cao, Qingxuan Wu, Zhiyang Dou, Rui Xu, Yuan Liu, Javier Fernandez-Marques, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Fine-tuning directly on user-side data is an effective way to improve the performance of widely adopted data-driven human mesh recovery (HMR) models. However, doing so for existing HMR methods often assumes aggregating user-side data from real-world HMR-deployed devices to a central training server, posing a significant privacy risk as sensitive human images are transmitted. How can human mesh recovery be evaluated and improved in privacy-constrained real-world settings? We study this question through a benchmark in which state-of-the-art HMR models are trained with federated learning and a secure-aggregation variant, neither of which centralizes raw images, and evaluated across a wide range of client-scale and data-heterogeneity settings. We document that common HMR training pipelines are built around centralized data access and quantify how representative HMR backbones behave when that assumption is removed. Furthermore, to study the data bottleneck of privacy-constrained HMR training, we propose DePoser, a local annotation and fine-tuning pipeline enhanced with depth foundation models, with which collaboratively trained HMR models can be locally tailored to the end user's distribution. On in-the-wild data, fine-tuning on DePoser annotations improves personalization accuracy, measured against DePoser-generated pseudo-ground truth. This benchmark aims to support future work on privacy-constrained HMR models and their real-world deployment and evaluation.
  </details>

- **[VTF900: A 900-Subject Visible-Thermal Face Recognition Dataset and Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24832)**  `arXiv:2610.24832`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jacob Abaare, Bianca Ianetta, Cedric Nimpa Fondje, Shuowen Hu, Benjamin S. Riggan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Despite advancements in visible-thermal face recognition, where thermal probe (query) face images are matched against visible enrollment templates, existing benchmarks are currently limited by small gallery sizes (N $\approx$ 100 subjects). This limitation inhibits the ability to accurately evaluate visible-thermal facial recognition at scale and to report significant recognition performance metrics, which have better operational relevance. Therefore, we introduce VTF900, a large-scale visible-thermal face recognition benchmark with 900 subjects and paired imagery that includes expression, pose, and occlusion variations. VTF900 is designed to support large-scale (i.e., large gallery) evaluations across verification (1:1 matching) and closed-set identification (1:N matching) for visible-thermal face recognition. Furthermore, VTF900 specifically addresses the challenging combination of cross-spectral and cross-dataset analysis. In this paper, after training models on the ARL-VTF dataset, we provide benchmark analyses across convolutional (baseline), vision transformers, and domain-adaptive neural network architectures to assess how these methods scale. At full gallery scale (N = 900), transformer models achieve strong discriminative performance under frontal conditions (e.g., ViT-B: 97.68% AUC, 51.10% TAR@1%FAR), while domain-adaptive methods such as DPIT-ResNet50 demonstrate greater robustness under off-pose conditions (94.68%AUC, 36.08% TAR@1%FAR). Our results show that high AUC at small scale does not translate to reliable performance at deployment scale, and that pose variation significantly amplifies performance degradation. VTF900 exposes these limitations, enabling more rigorous evaluation of cross-spectral face recognition systems.
  </details>

- **[SL-Ground: Sentence-Level Grounding for Fine-Grained Open-Vocabulary Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24814)**  `arXiv:2610.24814`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yang-Syu Li, Cheng-Huan Yang, Chia-Yung Jui, Peggy Joy Lu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Grounding-based open-vocabulary detectors provide a widely used framework for text-conditioned localization, with a phrase-grounding formulation that aligns object queries to individual text tokens and recovers class scores through token-to-class aggregation. We observe that this token-level scoring design becomes a bottleneck for fine-grained open-vocabulary object detection, where the target category is defined by a complete compositional description rather than by isolated tokens. Based on this observation, we propose SL-Ground, a sentence-level grounding reformulation for grounding-based open-vocabulary detection. Instead of scoring queries against fragmented token evidence, SL-Ground encodes each category description as a complete sentence-level representation and directly scores object queries against these representations. To evaluate fine-grained recognition across label granularity, we construct a multi-granularity Stanford Cars benchmark spanning vehicle type, brand, and car model. At the finest car-model granularity, SL-Ground raises AP over the token-level variant from 14.5 to 70.6 under matched training candidate sampling and to 86.0 with full-vocabulary training, while better preserving open-vocabulary capability and avoiding repeated prompt chunking. Beyond Stanford Cars, SL-Ground also improves FG-OVD performance, achieving the best average AP among compared methods. These results show that sentence-level grounding provides a more suitable scoring unit for fine-grained OVOD and offers a practical path toward fine-grained grounding-based open-vocabulary detectors.
  </details>

- **[Video-Language Entailment for Video Similarity Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24786)**  `arXiv:2610.24786`  `cs.CV`  
  _Benedetta Liberatori, Lorenzo Vaquero, Elisa Ricci, Paolo Rota_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Comparing videos to assess their semantic similarity remains a challenging task for current Large Multimodal Models (LMMs), which are primarily trained with single-video objectives. While most prior works have focused on computing global similarity, recent studies have introduced the more challenging problem of concept-based similarity, which measures how similar two videos are with respect to specific concepts (e.g., activity, location). However, no dedicated approaches have yet leveraged LMMs to explicitly address this task. In this paper, we aim to fill this gap introducing Video Caption Entailment for video similarity estimation (VICE): a dual-branch design that leverages LMMs for cross-video reasoning. Each branch jointly processes one video and the caption of the other, measuring their alignment for a specific concept via video-language entailment. The outputs from both branches are fused to produce a final similarity score, along with a textual explanation of the model reasoning. This approach leverages the model's native single-video understanding while enabling fine-grained comparative reasoning between video and text. Experiments on ConViS-Bench and StepDiff show that VICE outperforms models that process both videos simultaneously, highlighting the promise of single-video reasoning for concept-based similarity estimation.
  </details>

- **[ATLAS: Learning to Adapt LVLM Attention for Reasoning Video Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24783)**  `arXiv:2610.24783`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hamidreza Dastmalchi, Ali Cheraghian, Aijun An, Abdelwahed Khamis, Lars Petersson_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reasoning Video Object Segmentation (RVOS) aims to localize and segment objects referred to by natural-language expressions throughout a video. Although large vision-language models (LVLMs) implicitly encode localization cues in their attention maps, these attentions are often noisy, diffuse, and scattered across layers, heads, and modalities, limiting their direct use for segmentation. Existing methods typically rely on handcrafted attention-selection heuristics or computationally expensive attention refinement. We propose ATLAS, an attention adaptation framework that learns to transform raw LVLM attentions into object-centric localization cues. ATLAS treats attentions from all layers, heads, and modalities as structured features, combining high-resolution frame-level attentions that capture spatial information with low-resolution video-level attentions that encode temporal context. A lightweight U-Net equipped with temporal attention and FiLM-based modulation fuses these complementary representations to generate coarse segmentation heatmaps, which are propagated across the video using SAM2. Extensive experiments on challenging referring and reasoning video segmentation benchmarks show that ATLAS outperforms existing frozen-backbone methods. Code and models are available at \url{this https URL}.
  </details>

- **[Design Anything, Design at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24771)**  `arXiv:2610.24771`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xuchao Ren, Siyuan Feng, Jing Huang, Yanhua Yao, Jing Wang, Lingyu Alina Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  As multimodal resources are transformed into media outputs, agentic AI is expected to stay aligned with design intent over long horizons. Designers follow briefs specifying goals, constraints, and deliverables, with later work building on earlier decisions. Recent work reuses design experience across tasks, yet alignment among artifacts within one project remains challenging. Agents therefore need to preserve and apply this intent across domains and as projects grow. We introduce Design Anything, a graph-based harness that preserves design intent outside the model and binds deliverables to the workflow steps that produce them through an intent-execution graph. A task contract retains constraints and decisions, selected designs become explicit inputs to later generations, and a deterministic controller uses bindings and execution records to check output types and counts, identify outstanding deliverables, and guide subsequent turns. We also introduce DesignAnything-Bench, with 228 tasks across 19 design domains, including 38 project families at scales of 6, 14, and 24 artifacts. With the LLM held fixed, Design Anything satisfies 85.0% and 92.5% of the brief's factual constraints with DeepSeek V4.1 Flash High and GPT-5.6 Sol High, respectively, exceeding the strongest same-model baselines by 15.4% and 14.4%. These gains reach 25.1% and 23.0% on 24-artifact tasks. Under each model, Design Anything leads or ties in constraint satisfaction in 18 of 19 domains. Professional designers also prefer its complete deliverable sets over those of same-model baselines in a blind evaluation.
  </details>

- **[CorrCLIPv2: Harnessing Unlabeled Images for Open-Vocabulary Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24757)**  `arXiv:2610.24757`  `cs.CV`  
  _Dengke Zhang, Fagui Liu, Quan Tang, C. L. Philip Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Open-vocabulary semantic segmentation aims to classify pixels without being restricted to a fixed set of categories. Although Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training (CLIP) demonstrates remarkable potential for open-vocabulary recognition, its segmentation capability is impaired by noisy inter-class patch interactions. To this end, this work proposes a unified training-free object-centric framework, named CorrCLIPv2, from three complementary aspects of patch correlation, feature representation, and output regularization. Specifically, for patch correlation, we leverage class-agnostic mask generators to explicitly disentangle inter-class interactions. Moreover, we utilize spatially-coherent features from self-supervised models to compute attention scores to mitigate mask inaccuracies. Regarding feature representation, we devise a refinement mechanism to simultaneously strengthen spatial details and semantic representation. Within the output space, we unify predictions via voting in each mask to enhance spatial consistency. To mitigate contextual bias and enhance prediction accuracy of objects, we further extend the framework by incorporating a cross-image object retrieval paradigm, which incurs minimal computational overhead. We build a memory bank of CorrCLIP-enhanced features from unlabeled images and implement a regime of multi-CLIP ensemble. By aggregating semantically consistent object features across diverse contexts retrieved from the memory bank, CorrCLIPv2 mitigates context-induced interference and obtains more robust predictions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our approach establishes a new state-of-the-art on 10 standard benchmarks. Remarkably, it even surpasses fully supervised open-vocabulary methods across 22 multi-domain datasets. Code is available at:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Editing Images into Reusable Layers via Compositional Edit-Prior Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24738)**  `arXiv:2610.24738`  `cs.CV`  
  _Wen Xue, Yong Liu, Xueheng Li, Si Wu, Yong Xu, Yipeng Sun, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  High-quality image editing requires precise modification of target regions while preserving the content of unrelated regions. Existing editing models produce flattened outputs that limit precise local manipulation and layer reuse, while decomposition methods typically optimize layer recovery separately from instruction-driven editing. To this end, we introduce LayerEditor, a unified framework that couples instruction-guided image editing with controllable generation of reusable RGBA layers. At its core, In-Context RGB-Alpha Token Modeling jointly represents the edited canvas and an ordered sequence of RGB-Alpha layers within a single token sequence, enabling semantic edits and explicit control over individual layer content. To retain the pretrained model's editing capability during adaptation to layered outputs, we propose Compositional Edit-Prior Distillation, which transfers its editing prior to both the edited canvas and the image composed from the predicted layers. To support training and evaluation, we construct LayerEditor-100K, a large-scale paired image-editing dataset with ordered RGBA layer annotations, and introduce dedicated benchmarks for layered editing. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LayerEditor maintains strong editing fidelity while generating accurate RGBA layers, supporting both flexible layer-level manipulation and multi-layer decomposition.
  </details>

- **[From Views to Worlds: Active Exploration over 3D Worlds for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24676)**  `arXiv:2610.24676`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qijian Tian, Jiayu Ying, Ke Fan, Lizhuang Ma, Xin Tan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language models (VLMs) have achieved remarkable progress in 2D visual understanding tasks, yet the passive view-centric perception paradigm limits their capacity for 3D spatial reasoning. Such reasoning often requires actively acquiring spatial evidence beyond the currently visible views, such as camera poses and cross-view spatial relationships, to infer 3D relationships that are not explicitly represented in 2D images. However, existing methods either lack active exploration or merely provide additional views during inference, without explicitly acquiring such spatial evidence beyond 2D images. In this paper, we propose View-to-World (V2W), an active exploration framework that enables VLMs to actively explore 3D worlds and lifts VLMs from passive view-centric perception to active world-centric spatial reasoning. Given a spatial reasoning task with multi-view images, V2W first reconstructs an explicit 3D world with geometry, camera poses, and language-grounded semantics, and exposes it to VLMs through visual and linguistic interfaces. Through multi-turn interaction with these interfaces, VLMs iteratively acquire spatial evidence from the constructed 3D world, enabling active world-centric spatial reasoning. V2W can be integrated with existing VLMs in a training-free manner and can be further enhanced by optimizing the exploration policy via reinforcement learning. Experiments on spatial mental modeling benchmarks demonstrate that V2W substantially improves VLM baselines in the training-free setting and achieves state-of-the-art performance with the learned exploration policy, highlighting the importance of active exploration for spatial intelligence. Project page: \url{this https URL}.
  </details>

- **[Spectral-Aware Proposal Enhancement for Diffusion-Based Multispectral UAV Multi-Object Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24642)**  `arXiv:2610.24642`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xu Lin, Tingfa Xu, Tianhao Li, Haolin Qin, Huawei Gao, Jichuan Xing, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multispectral UAV multi-object tracking (MOT) with oriented bounding boxes remains challenging due to small targets, cluttered backgrounds, and unstable inter-frame associations. Although diffusion-based MOT provides an effective iterative proposal refinement paradigm, existing diffusion-based trackers lack dedicated designs for exploiting multispectral cues in proposal initialization and confidence calibration, limiting tracking robustness in complex UAV scenarios. This letter proposes DSATrack, a diffusion-guided spectral-aware tracking framework for multispectral UAV MOT. Specifically, a lightweight spectral encoder injects multispectral information into the RPN branch through feature fusion, enabling the RPN head to generate high-quality object-aware proposals. These proposals are then injected into the initial proposal set to provide reliable spatial priors for diffusion refinement. Furthermore, a spectral-saliency confidence modulation strategy recalibrates refined tracking confidence according to local multispectral saliency contrast before score thresholding, enhancing spectrally reliable candidates while suppressing unreliable ones. Without modifying the original diffusion refinement head, DSATrack improves both proposal quality and post-refinement candidate selection. Experiments on the MMOT benchmark demonstrate that DSATrack outperforms the adapted DiffusionTrack baseline by 4.6 HOTA, 9.0 MOTA, and 7.7 IDF1 points, showing its effectiveness for multispectral UAV MOT.
  </details>

- **[IntriAdapt: Generalizable Intrinsic-Guided Test-Time Adaptation for Metric Geometry Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24607)**  `arXiv:2610.24607`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiaxing Chen, Zizheng Yan, Jingyu Zhuang, Jinwei Chen, Shuguang Cui, Guanying Chen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recovering metric 3D geometry from images is fundamentally ill-posed due to perspective projection ambiguity. Current geometry models incorporate supplied or predicted intrinsics during inference, but do not use them to supervise model adaptation. To refine these predictions, test-time adaptation (TTA) typically relies on external depth measurements. In this paper, we instead use camera intrinsics as an output-side geometric verifier. Comparing the point cloud's viewing rays with those defined by the known intrinsics yields a direct measure of geometric inconsistency. We propose \textit{IntriAdapt}, a TTA framework that minimizes this closed-form geometric residual. By optimizing a lightweight LoRA adapter on a frozen geometry foundation model, our method aligns the prediction with the specific camera model without depth sensors or external priors. We further extend this approach to video sequences, demonstrating that adapting a short sequence generalizes to other sequences captured by the same camera. The adapted model processes held-out images in a feed-forward pass, without repeating optimization for each frame. Experiments on ten single-image and seven sequence benchmarks across multiple base models show that \textit{IntriAdapt} consistently outperforms both unadapted and oracle-intrinsics baselines in metric accuracy and relative geometry.
  </details>

- **[An Optical Music Recognition Tool for String Quartets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24530)**  `arXiv:2610.24530`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jin-Shiang Hu, Bo-Yuan Cheng, Li Su_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  This paper presents an optical music recognition (OMR) system tailored for PDF scores of string quartet pieces. The approach begins with a SegNet-based segmentation model to localize musical regions, followed by convolutional neural networks and image processing techniques to classify and extract musical symbols. To improve rhythmic precision, dynamic programming methods enforce time signature constraints and explicitly handle tuplets. Evaluation using a longest common subsequence metric across multiple feature matrices demonstrates robust accuracy in recognizing pitch, onset, duration, and accidentals on a benchmark dataset. Code and data are available atthis https URL.
  </details>
